ParlaMint-SI_2016-06-21-SDZ7-Redna-20.ana.u93	Thank you very much. For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government of Mrs Tina Brecelj, State Secretary at the Ministry of Justice. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-06-21-SDZ7-Redna-20.ana.u94	Thank you very much, Chairman, for your words. Good-bye, everybody! The Group of Members and Members, as has already been said, has submitted a similar proposal and, in a given description, a proposal to supplement the Penal Code to the National Assembly already in 2014 and 2015. In both cases, in its reasoned opinions, the Government justified why the draft laws were not suitable for further consideration. This proposal is based again on United Nations Security Council Resolution No. 2178 in 2014, which is not sufficiently specific from the point of view of criminal substantive law. In doing so, they do not take into account the fact, and this is particularly important that the Council of Europe has adopted in the meantime and the Republic of Slovenia has signed an additional protocol to the Council of Europe Convention on the Prevention of Terrorism. It is also important for the future regulation of the area of consideration that the proposal for a Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on combating terrorism and replacing the Framework Council Decision on combating terrorism is in the process of being adopted. The proposed new 111a Article of the Penal Code highlights only a few ambiguities, but in particular I stress that the proposaler is also advised by the Council of the Court of Justice to define the crime. In the first paragraph of Article 111a proposed, inter alia, incriminating or recruiting, preparing, transporting and training individuals or groups to join or participate in a foreign armed association. At the same time, the applicant will overlook that, in the Penal Code, the offence of recruitment for terrorism is already established in Article 111, paragraph 1, in force. In accordance with the provisions of the general section of the Penal Code, the provision of Article 111 may also apply to a Slovenian citizen who commits such an act abroad, as well as to an alien who commits such an act abroad against a Slovenian citizen or the Republic of Slovenia, as well as against a foreigner or a foreign country, if it is caught in the territory of the Republic of Slovenia and is not handed over to a foreign country. Thus, the entry of a criminal offence in the proposed first paragraph of the new 111a is largely covered by the provisions of the applicable Penal Code. It is also quite unclear to determine the amount of the penalty. For the so-called preparatory acts, the proposed penalty is from 2 to 10 years in prison and for the basic form of the criminal offence, although we know that the preparatory acts are criminalized only exceptionally, i.e. for the basic form of the criminal offence, less than 6 months to 5 years in prison. The proposed third paragraph of the new 111a deals with duplication with the applicable provisions of the Penal Code. However, in the fourth paragraph proposed, there is no clear difference between the terms 'calling' or 'guarding'. So, in this paragraph, there's quite some inconsistencies. As I have already mentioned, Slovenia has signed an additional protocol to the Council of Europe Convention on the Prevention of Terrorism. It is this signature that binds us to a certain extent that the Penal Code still needs to be harmonised. The harmonisation will go towards the crime of accepting training for terrorism, which means that this part must be reintroduced, which is intended to do, as well as with regard to the offence of travelling abroad for the purpose of terrorism, which covers both the purpose of service or participation in the service of terrorist offences and the purpose of offering or accepting training for terrorism and the crime of financing such travel. Even with the signing of the Protocol, the Penal Code will be somewhat harmonised in the future. However, as I have said, in the main part, the proposals made by the proposer or covered by the proposer are already covered in our legislation, already criminalised. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-06-21-SDZ7-Redna-20.ana.u142	Thank you very much for your words, Chairman. The distinguished representative of the Government, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The Law on Integrity and Prevention of Corruption in Article 22, paragraph 4, provides that the President of the Republic may, on a proposal from the National Assembly, dismiss the President of the Commission for the Prevention of Corruption if he fails to perform functions in accordance with the Constitution and the law. In doing so, it should be pointed out that the content of the provision allowing for the early resolution of the President of the Commission in order to prevent corruption must be understood within the meaning of the principle of the division of power. An essential element of this principle is the Institute of Brakes and Balance, which establishes a constitutionally coherent balance between the institutions of different functions. The main responsibility of the President of the Commission for the prevention of corruption is to represent the Commission and to lead and organise its work. Its resolution may only take place for reasons laid down in the tax ruling and it is imperative that the existence of specific infringements of the Constitution or the law be determined before the decision on discharge is taken. Such a decision must not be politically or legally unrelated. The reasons given by the applicant in his decision are such. The alleged violations contained in the applicant's material cannot be said to be specific breaches of the Constitution and the law. Nor can it be argued that these alleged violations of the Constitution and of the Commission President's law to prevent corruption have caused any harmful consequences that would require the exercise of responsibility. An individual may only be held responsible for his actions if this is proved to him in the manner prescribed by the law. By adopting the Law on Integrity and Prevention of Corruption, the legislator appointed one body responsible for overseeing the officials of all three branches of government and other independent and independent state bodies. For this reason, it is all the more important that this body therefore enjoys its independent and independent position in order to prevent corruption. Members and Members of the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre are of the view that the proposal does not justify a discharge when the President of the Republic, on a proposal from the National Assembly, can dismiss the President of the Commission, so we could not support such a proposal. Thank you very much.

The draft law was tabled by the Government. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the State Secretary at the Ministry of Environment and the premises of Mrs Lidija Stebernak.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-06-21-SDZ7-Redna-20.ana.u207	Honourable President of the National Assembly, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! Allow me to present to you, on behalf of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia, the reasons for the adoption of the Proposal for a Law on the Ratification of the Treaty between the Republic of Slovenia and Hungary on the adaptation of the State border line due to the water management regime of the Ledava River. In the introduction, let me explain the reasons that led to the drafting of the bill. In accordance with the current International Treaty on the State Border, the State border between the Republic of Slovenia and Hungary takes place at a six-kilometre section along the centre of the Ledava stream. In the past, this watercourse has often flooded and meandered between fields, thus altering the course of its stream. As a result, the watercourse in this section was regulated and surrounded between 1994 and 1998 by appropriate flood embankments. Due to this regulation of the Ledova River border stream, there was a situation at this section of the Slovenian-Hungarian state border where the border line is not now in nature running through the middle of the regulated stream, but a branch of the stream, partly on the embankments on both sides of the watercourse. Such a situation makes it difficult to access and process land at the state border and to keep the embankments on both sides on a regulated stream stream. At the meetings of the mixed Slovenian-Hungarian State Border Maintenance Commission, the initiative to change the flow of the national border at this section was considered immediately after the regulation and the water management regime of the Ledava River, in order to allow the state border to take place in the middle of the amended watercourse under regulation. Between 2001 and 2006, competent services in both countries developed technical and technical bases for the implementation of the adjustment of the State border line due to the water management regime of the stream. A proposal for a new state border flow at this section has been drawn up so that the state border runs through the middle of the new stream, bearing in mind that the number of Slovenian pieces of land is the same as the sum of Hungarian pieces of land that will pass from the territory of one neighbouring country to the territory of the other country in the event of a change in the course of the state border. The proposed amendment should be understood as a measure which is dictated by the situation in nature as an adaptation of the border line to the field conditions. This shall amend the border course without prejudice to one or the other Contracting Party. On 22 January 2016, the Minister for Foreign Affairs of the Republic of Slovenia and the Minister for the Interior of Hungary signed the contract at Brdo pri Kranju. The Hungarian side has already completed the ratification process before its Parliament, which published the contract by law on 12 April 2016. On 9 May 2016, the Slovenian Government laid down a proposal for a law on the ratification of the Treaty and forwarded it to the National Assembly. Following the adoption of the Law on the ratification of the Treaty between the Republic of Slovenia and Hungary on the adaptation of the State border line due to the water management regime of the Ledava River, the exchange of diplomatic notes between the countries will follow and the law will enter into force. The mixed Slovenian-Hungarian State Border Maintenance Commission will record the agreed amendment of the state border in the border documentation and ensure that the state border is properly marked with border signs in nature. Thank you.

POINT OF THE DAY’S LINE – THIS IS A DEFINITIVE PROPOSAL FOR THE RATIFICATION OF THE ATHENS CONVENTION ON THE TRANSPORT OF PASSENGERS AND THEIR MARRIAGE BY SEA FROM 1974 AND THE 2002 PROTOCOL TO THE ATHENS CONVENTION ON THE TRANSPORT OF PASSENGERS AND THEIR CARRIAGE BY THE SEA FROM 1974. The draft law was tabled by the Government. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary at the Ministry of Infrastructure mag. I swear to Grebenko.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-06-21-SDZ7-Redna-20.ana.u209	Thank you so much for saying that. Dear Members and Members! The proposal to ratify the Athens Convention on the Transport of Passengers and Their Luggage by Sea of 1974 and the Protocol of 2002 to this Convention fall within the group of international contracts concerning the general limitation of the carrier's liability for damage suffered by a passenger on board the ship. The carrier shall be liable for damage caused by the death or physical injury of a passenger resulting from a maritime accident and for damage caused by the loss or damage to the luggage if the accident causing the damage occurred during the carriage on purpose or because of the negligence of the carrier. The Convention also provides for the possibility of limiting the liability of the carrier, the actual carrier and the persons working for the carrier to claims for death, physical injury, loss and damage to cabin baggage and the loss and damage to vehicles, including all luggage loaded. The limitation of liability for those claims cannot be enforced if it is demonstrated that the damage is due to premeditation or gross negligence. The 2002 Protocol to the 1974 Athens Convention substantially amends the basic Convention and improves the liability of carriers and compensation for passengers in maritime transport and introduces compulsory insurance in favour of passengers not less than the current value of EUR 310 000 for passengers. However, States parties to the Protocol may also lay down a higher liability limit by means of national legislation. While the European Union has transposed most of the provisions of the Athens Convention as amended by the 2002 Protocol into the legal order of the European Union by Regulation 392/2009 on the liability of carriers of passengers by sea and in case of accidents, but as a result of the wider election, we have been invited by the EU Member States by the European Commission to accede to the 2002 Protocol as soon as possible, so I propose that the National Assembly adopt the Act ratifying the Athens Convention on the Transport of Passengers and Their Luggage by Sea, 1974 and the 2002 Protocol to this Convention. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-06-21-SDZ7-Redna-20.ana.u210	The draft law was treated by the Committee on Foreign Policy as a mother labourer who reported in writing to the assembly. As no amendment was adopted by the parent working body during the second reading, no amendments could be tabled at the assembly meeting. That is why we are moving on to a decision on the bill. We're voting. There are 68 Members and Members present, with 68 votes against and no one against. [[The vote was 68.]] [[No one.]] I take it the law has been passed. Matei Vranictor Erman.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-06-21-SDZ7-Redna-20.ana.u211	Mr President, thank you for the floor. Ladies and gentlemen, Members. International double taxation, which is due to the overlap between tax laws of different countries, is hampering international economic cooperation. Its creation is best prevented by the conclusion of bilateral conventions on the avoidance of double taxation, which are now a common way of international bargaining in this area. The Conventions remove tax barriers to trade and investment between countries which conclude the Convention and reduce the possibility of tax evasion. Currently, 57 double taxation conventions apply between Slovenia and other States Parties. Slovenia's negotiations for the conclusion of such conventions are based on a draft convention prepared on the basis of the OECD Model Convention on the Prevention of Double Taxation and the Prevention of Tax Misuses in respect of Income and Property Taxes. The views submitted by Slovenia to individual articles of the Model Convention shall also be taken into account. The Convention, which is ratified today, was signed in Astana on 10 March 2016. It was signed for Slovenia by Ambassador Primož Šeligo and for Kazakhstan by the Minister of Finance of the Republic of Kazakhstan Bahit Sultanov. The Convention, through various mechanisms, allows for the elimination of double taxation, increases the security of taxpayers, prevents tax discrimination, allows the resolution of tax disputes, as well as the exchange of information for tax purposes and assistance in tax collection. We propose that the proposed ratification law be supported. Thank you very much.

Article 130 of the Constitution provides that the Judges shall be elected by the National Assembly on a proposal from the Council of Justice. In accordance with the provisions of Article 28 of the Judicial Service Act, the Council shall propose to the National Assembly candidates for election to judicial office. The same law in the second paragraph of Article 36 provides that a judge shall serve as a judge in the court for which he has run for office and in the judicial seat for which he has been elected or appointed, unless otherwise provided for in the law. The Law on Judicial Service in the second paragraph of Article 19 provides that the Council of the State shall propose to the Assembly for election for each vacant seat for which a judge, one candidate, is to be elected after the selection procedure and, in accordance with the first paragraph of Article 20 of this Law, shall be deemed to be appointed by election to the judicial office of the judge.
